Fixed by rebalancing margin against the real price at each funding period (`klines_df["close"].asof(ts)`) instead of a stale fixed snapshot — re-verified zero liquidation events at the chosen leverage after the fix, this time for a real reason rather than an artifact.
    - **Result**: BTC +51.6% (Sharpe 9.04), ETH +55.3% (Sharpe 8.45), both over the full available history, no liquidation events at the sizing above. New tables `funding_arb_backtests`/`funding_arb_equity_curves` (`store/funding_arb.py`) log every run the same way the rest of the platform's backtests do.
    - **Paper trading built and verified against real data, not just read back from stored state**: `funding_arb_paper.py`'s `check_and_update()` opened a real position, correctly no-op'd when nothing new had settled (confirmed by checking Bitnomial's raw timestamps directly rather than trusting the no-op silently), and correctly reconstructed 14 real settled funding periods plus a correct cumulative P&L after a temporary backdated-entry test — new `funding_arb_paper_events` table, append-only, deliberately separate from the existing `paper_trade_events` table since that one is entry/exit-timed and this strategy has no exit, only continuous funding accrual plus a liquidation check.
    - **Scheduled live via `launchd`** (`com.quantplatform.fundingarbpapertrade.plist`, loaded and running, checks in every 8 hours matching the exchange's own funding interval) — confirmed firing correctly against the real schedule, not just loaded. First fully non-directional, always-on strategy the platform runs unattended.
    - **Update 2026-08-02 — ETH added, and a real Bitnomial price-scaling bug found and fixed along the way.** Asked to add ETH to the schedule; finding its Bitnomial symbol (`ETHUI`) led to sanity-checking its live price against the exchange's own real-time markets page, which didn't match — and the same check on `BTCUC` (already live since 2026-07-30) found the identical mismatch, off by roughly 5x. Root cause: Bitnomial's funding-rates API returns price as a raw tick count, not USD — `fetch_bitnomial_history()` had been storing that raw value directly. Fixed via each product's real `price_increment` (found through the exchange's own `/prod/product/specs` endpoint: BTC=5, ETH=0.2; increment-corrected values then matched the live site within ~1%). **Checked the actual blast radius before calling it fixed**: position sizing is a fixed dollar split of `init_cash`, funding P&L is `rate × notional`, and the liquidation check compares Binance klines to Binance klines — none of those ever touch Bitnomial's `mark_price`, and the backtest (item 93's BTC +51.6%/ETH +55.3% numbers) uses Binance's own funding history, not Bitnomial's at all. So the strategy's real economics were never affected — the bug was confined to the recorded `price` field in the audit-trail tables, corrected via a one-time `UPDATE` on all 229 already-stored rows plus the 8 already-logged BTC paper-trading events, rather than left wrong going forward. ETH opened for real ($1,845 entry, notional $6,000, margin $4,000 at 1.5x) and both coins now run through the same existing `launchd` schedule via `funding_arb_paper_trade.sh` — no new plist needed. Full writeup in [[Enhancement Ideas]].
    - **Same session, second gap closed: the maintenance margin rate was never actually confirmed with Bitnomial, and the real number is 30x the placeholder.** Asked directly whether liquidation risk was truly zero or just unlikely — answering honestly meant finally confirming `MAINTENANCE_MARGIN_RATE` (0.005, borrowed from a different exchange, flagged in its own code comment as unconfirmed since the original scoping). Found Bitnomial's real, live figure via the same product-data endpoint (`product_group_margin_rate: 0.15`, cross-checked against their published SPAN-style margin methodology) — 15%, not 0.5%. Re-ran the full 6.5-year backtest with the corrected rate before reporting anything reassuring: **zero liquidation events for both BTC and ETH, unchanged.** The "survives every real crash" conclusion holds under the actual confirmed number, not just the borrowed one. Updated the constant in code and re-recorded the official backtest run.

94. **Fixed a real, meaningful bug in every long/short strategy's conflict-resolution logic — turned out to affect 21 of 164 currently-PROMOTE `_ls` combos, including 2 of the 3 live paper-traded ones, not just the one crashing ticker that surfaced it (2026-08-03)** — investigating `FFAI/breakout_ls`'s known `SizeType.Percent does not support position reversal` crash (flagged in passing during the item-90-gap refresh) led somewhere much bigger than a one-ticker fix.
    - **The old fix (`backtest/run.py`) was never actually correct, just usually lucky.** It masked `exits & short_entries` (and the mirror) and shifted the conflicting entry forward one bar — but read vectorbt's own source (`nb.py`'s `signals_to_size_nb`) to confirm directly: vectorbt's reversal check fires whenever `short_entries` is True while the position is *actually* long, full stop, regardless of whether `exits` is also True that bar. For a strategy whose entry/exit windows are nested (`DonchianBreakoutStrategy`: exit_window < entry_window, mathematically guaranteeing `short_entries` implies `exits` on *every* day of a breakdown, not just the first), a persistent multi-day signal run just shifts the same unresolved conflict forward by one bar each time — it only avoided crashing before because every prior run happened to be exactly 1 day long, not because the logic was right.
    - **The real fix is a genuine bar-by-bar position walk** (`_resolve_reversal_conflicts_nb()`, numba-jitted for speed at scale), tracking actual simulated flat/long/short state instead of a static boolean mask — the only way to know what vectorbt's own check actually depends on. Verified directly: FFAI/breakout_ls truncated to end just before its own data artifact (below) now runs cleanly end-to-end with real, sane stats (153.8% return, 34 trades) where it previously crashed on the very first isolated-conflict case the old code was ever asked to handle correctly.
    - **FFAI/breakout_ls itself still fails — for a completely different, newly-discovered reason, reported honestly rather than papered over.** Bisected the exact crash date: `FFAI`'s raw close jumps from $0.112 to $10.80 (96x) on 2026-07-23. Confirmed via `yfinance`'s own `Ticker.splits` this is a real 1-for-150 reverse split (ratio 0.006667, dated 2026-07-24) — but `auto_adjust=True` and `auto_adjust=False` return *identical* unadjusted values, meaning yfinance itself hasn't back-applied this very recent split to historical closes yet. A short position held across that gap has to "cover" at 96x the price it was shorted at with unchanged share count — a genuine, if data-driven, wipeout that correctly can't complete as a same-bar reversal. This is a distinct data-quality gap (yfinance split-adjustment lag for very recent corporate actions), not a code bug in this platform, and not fixed here — flagged for whoever picks it up next.
    - **Checked the blast radius before calling this done, not just the one ticker**: scanned every currently-PROMOTE `_ls`-family combo (164 of 1,132) for the old code's residual unresolved conflicts — **21 combos affected**, including `DHC/volume_breakout_ls` (currently live) and `QQQ/breakout_ls` (also live, already a known item-38 risk-gate exception). Concrete before/after for the two live combos with real prior conflicts: `DHC/volume_breakout_ls` +428.3%/Sharpe 1.251/DD -24.4%/48 trades → **+533.5%/Sharpe 1.310/DD -25.0%/48 trades**; `QQQ/breakout_ls` -28.9%/Sharpe -0.202/DD -43.7%/90 trades → **-28.7%/Sharpe -0.184/DD -48.1%/92 trades**. `BLCO/bollinger_ls` (the third live LS combo) had zero old-code conflicts and is essentially unchanged, as expected.
    - **Not acted on beyond the code fix itself, on purpose**: did not rewrite `backtest_runs`/`promotion_snapshots` for the 21 affected combos or touch the live paper-trading roster — the corrected DHC/QQQ numbers above are real but come from an ad-hoc check, not an official research-cycle run, and changing what's live is exactly the kind of consequential call this project's own "propose, a human decides" principle (see below) says shouldn't happen without the user reviewing the actual numbers first, same as item 90's demotions. **Recommend**: re-run `research_cycle.py` (or a scoped equivalent) for the `_ls` family to get honest, official updated verdicts before trusting the current promoted/live numbers for any of the 21 affected combos.
    - **Follow-up, same day**: real refresh run confirmed both live combos with prior conflicts (`DHC/volume_breakout_ls`, `BLCO/bollinger_ls`) genuinely fail the promotion bar under the corrected logic — pulled from live paper trading, see the "Suggested next steps" entry above for the real before/after numbers.

95. **Ran a fresh `research_cycle.py` cycle on the curated 32-ticker universe (640 combos) — first full cycle since the backtest fix, not just a scoped re-check (2026-08-03)** — 22 combos newly cleared the promotion bar, 8 demoted, 1,106 unchanged, 80 evaluated for the first time (mostly `kusha_breakout`/`kusha_breakout_ls` on tickers never tested with it before, all landing HOLD). Most of the 30 verdict changes are the correlation gate re-sorting winners as new aggressive-track siblings cleared the bar this cycle, the same pattern items 90/94 already documented, not fresh walk-forward failures.
    - **Two of the 4 currently-live paper-traded combos technically demoted, but not for the reason that matters** — checked the real before/after numbers before reporting this as concerning, same discipline as every other demotion this session: `FXI/bollinger_regime`'s own worst-OOS Sharpe (0.279057 → 0.279058) and mean DSR (0.635 → 0.628) barely moved at all; it lost the correlation gate to `FXI/bollinger_regime_aggressive` (0.93 correlated, marginally higher DSR) newly clearing the bar this cycle. Same story for `QQQ/breakout` (worst-OOS Sharpe 0.552 → 0.553, essentially unchanged) — outranked by `SPY/breakout` (0.72 correlated, marginally higher DSR). **Neither combo's own edge weakened** — this is the correlation gate doing exactly what it's designed to do (prefer the higher-DSR of two very similar bets), not a sign either one stopped working. Genuinely different in character from item 94's `BLCO`/`DHC` demotions, which were real walk-forward failures.
    - **Decided, same day: leave both as-is.** User's call — `FXI/bollinger_regime` and `QQQ/breakout` stay in live paper trading despite the technical correlation-gate HOLD, on the same footing as `QQQ/breakout_ls`'s existing item-38 exception. No code change needed (a correlation-gate HOLD was never auto-removing anything — `daily_paper_trade.sh`'s roster is hardcoded, only ever edited by hand, same principle as every other roster change this session). Worth revisiting only if a future cycle finds either combo's *own* worst-OOS Sharpe genuinely deteriorating, not just losing another correlation tiebreak.

96. **The Mac mini migration turned out to already be effectively done — and checking that surfaced a real, silent DB-backup corruption bug, found and fixed the same night (2026-08-03)** — asked to work on "bigger stuff," picked Mac mini migration first. Checking the actual current state before planning anything (rather than assuming) found every `com.quantplatform.*` launchd job already loaded and running on the Mac mini exclusively, power/sleep settings already correctly configured for always-on operation (`sleep 0`, `standby 0`, `autorestart 1`), and the old MacBook confirmed (by the user) to no longer run any of these jobs — no duplicate-execution or diverging-database risk. The one real gap found: auto-login isn't enabled, so an unattended reboot leaves every scheduled job dead until someone logs in physically — correctly left to the user to set themselves (System Settings requires their account password, a system-security change I won't push through even when explicitly asked).
    - **A real, previously-unknown bug, found by actually verifying rather than trusting the existing "DB isn't backed up" note**: `backup_database.sh`'s daily iCloud backup (running since 2026-07-23) has been silently producing corrupt, unopenable snapshots. Confirmed directly: 2026-08-02 and both 2026-08-03 copies all fail with genuine DuckDB internal errors (`MarkBlockAsModified called with already freed block id`, `Failed to load metadata pointer`) — not a version/format issue (ruled out by testing read-write mode on a throwaway copy, same failure) and not a corrupted live database (verified separately and directly: 15,131,662 `ohlcv_daily` rows, opens and queries cleanly, completely healthy).
    - **Root cause**: the backup script did a plain `cp` of the live `.duckdb` file, and its own code comments already anticipated the exact risk — safe only if "nothing writes to this database continuously." That assumption silently broke once `agent_ecosystem.py` became a persistent, always-on dashboard service (item 51) rather than a one-shot scheduled job — it can read or write the database at any moment, including the 06:00 backup window, and a raw file copy that catches a concurrent write produces exactly this kind of torn, unreadable file. Nobody would have found out until an actual restore was needed.
    - **Fixed with DuckDB's own `ATTACH` + `COPY FROM DATABASE`** run against a read-only connection to the live database — takes a real consistent snapshot regardless of concurrent activity, the actual fix rather than a narrower scheduling assumption that could just break again the same way. Written to a `.tmp` path and only renamed into place after a real verification query (open the result, count a real table's rows) succeeds, so a failed or silently-corrupt backup can never again sit under a normal dated filename looking exactly as trustworthy as a good one — closing the exact gap that let this go unnoticed for over a week. Tested end-to-end against the real live database, not just a synthetic case: produced and independently re-verified a genuine backup (15,131,662 rows, matching the live database exactly).
    - **Not touched, deliberately**: the older, unverified pre-fix snapshots already on disk. No manual cleanup needed — the existing 14-day retention naturally prunes them out as verified backups accumulate daily going forward.

97. **Added a daily portfolio-level drawdown check with a desktop alert at -15% (2026-08-03)** — closes a gap flagged since early in the project ("daily loss limits / portfolio-level exposure — needs a multi-strategy or live-loop context that doesn't exist yet"). That blocker is gone: 4 price-trend combos plus BTC/ETH funding-arb now run concurrently, a genuine multi-strategy live loop. Every individual combo already has its own backtest-time risk gate (-25% drawdown); nothing had ever watched the *live* book day to day.
    - **Two separate checks, not one forced blend** — matching the existing "not blended, not interchangeable" convention `funding_arb_paper.py` already established between price-trend and funding-arb: (1) the 4 live price-trend combos, read directly from `research_cycle.py`'s `CURRENTLY_PAPER_TRADED` so this can never drift out of sync with the real roster, reconstructed from real `paper_trade_events` (not a backtest) and blended equal-weight; (2) each funding-arb symbol independently from its own real accrual events, since it has a different capital basis and continuous-accrual mechanics rather than discrete trades.
    - **Fact-only, same "automation proposes, a human decides" principle as everything else here**: never touches a position or `CURRENTLY_PAPER_TRADED`, only fires a desktop notification (same `osascript` mechanism `stock_agent.py` already uses) if either side's real drawdown from its own peak breaches -15%. New `scripts/check_portfolio_drawdown.py`, wired into `daily_paper_trade.sh` as the last step so it reads the same day's fresh state.
    - **Tested against real live data, not synthetic**: confirmed correct output via the standalone script and a full real end-to-end `daily_paper_trade.sh` run (exit 0) — current state as of tonight, `-0.88%` on the price-trend book (only `QQQ/breakout_ls` has actually traded so far; the other 3 combos are still flat since inception), `0.00%` on both funding-arb legs. Also sent a one-off test notification through the same code path before trusting it — the call completed without error, but whether it was actually seen on screen hasn't been confirmed by the user yet.

98. **Volatility selling revisited and actually built, real data found where "no free source exists" had been the standing conclusion — real, honest HOLD result (2026-08-03)** — asked for something "big, new, and innovative." The old conclusion was true for a full options chain, but never actually required: the literature-standard way to harvest the volatility risk premium is trading the VIX term structure itself through a regular ETF, not synthesizing a position from individual strikes.
    - **New free data source, confirmed directly**: CBOE publishes VIX/VIX9D/VIX3M/SKEW daily closes as plain CSV, no key, no rate limit, full history (VIX back to 1990-01-02, VIX3M to 2009-09-18) — confirmed with a real request, not the docs. New `data/cboe_volatility.py`, reusing `data/fred.py`'s existing `macro_series` table/`store()`/`load()` rather than a new schema (the shape already fit exactly); its own `known_as_of()` is a same-day reindex, not FRED's revision-lag machinery, since CBOE's daily closes are never restated after the fact.
    - **New `strategies/vix_term_structure.py`**: long the ETP (`SVXY`, the standard short-vol instrument) only while VIX/VIX3M is in contango (the market's normal state, ~81% of days historically) with edge-triggered entries/exits and an entry/exit hysteresis band, same pattern as `turn_of_month.py` (no per-ticker price signal at all) and `DonchianBreakoutStrategy`'s separate entry/exit windows respectively. The platform's first volatility-selling strategy of any kind.
    - **Caught the exact same "unadjusted split inflates the number" trap item 94 hit hours earlier, checked before trusting anything**: `SVXY` buy-and-hold showing +453.5% over 2011-2026 looked suspicious for a fund that famously lost ~90% in one day (Feb 6, 2018, "Volmageddon"). Verified directly via `yfinance`'s own split history: the Feb 2018 move is a real, genuine crash (not a data artifact — `Stock Splits` column is 0 that week), and the later 1-for-4 reverse split (Sep 2018, a real post-crash fund reorganization) is 8 years settled and correctly adjusted by `data/fetch.py`'s existing dividend/split handling, unlike `FFAI`'s days-old split. The number is real.
    - **Directly verified the mechanism, not just the aggregate return**: checked the strategy's actual day-by-day position state against the two known real short-vol blowups — flat 80% of trading days during Volmageddon (Feb 2-6 2018) and 91% of trading days during the COVID crash (Feb 20-Mar 23 2020). Not a perfect top-tick exit (no signal-based system gets that), but genuinely, mostly out during both.
    - **Full risk picture, not just total return**: conservative-track full-history backtest: +209.7% return, Sharpe 0.711, max drawdown -30.4%, 63 trades, 34.9% win rate (low win rate, consistent with real short-vol's "many small wins, occasional larger losses" shape) — against plain `SVXY` buy-and-hold's +453.5% return, Sharpe 0.682, **max drawdown -95.2%**. Lower absolute return, but a *better* Sharpe and a dramatically better drawdown — the real tradeoff a stress-avoiding gate is supposed to produce, not a strictly worse strategy.
    - **Ran through the real pipeline both risk tracks, not just eyeballed the full-history number**: conservative track, real DSR = **0.825** (strong, well above the 0.5 threshold) but HOLD — fails `full_history_risk_passed` (-30.4% exceeds the conservative -25% gate) and `all_oos_windows_positive` (one of 4 walk-forward windows shows a genuine negative out-of-sample Sharpe, -0.230). Aggressive track (looser -50% drawdown gate, higher target vol/leverage): clears the risk gate this time and **beats SPY outright** (+424.3% vs +346.5%), but still HOLD — same walk-forward-window failure, plus falls just short of its own leveraged buy-and-hold.
    - **Honest verdict, not spun**: a real, statistically significant edge (DSR 0.825) with a genuinely favorable risk-adjusted profile versus naive buy-and-hold, that does not currently clear this platform's own promotion bar on either track. Not promoted, not paper-traded. Code committed regardless — real, reusable infrastructure (a genuinely new, non-price-technical data source and the platform's first volatility-selling strategy) independent of today's specific verdict, same precedent as `kusha_stateful.py`/pairs-trading follow-ups staying in the codebase after a research pass that didn't immediately promote.

99. **Asked to try a different angle on item 98's HOLD, and the second angle actually cleared the bar for real — `SPY/breakout_vixgated` is a genuine new PROMOTE (2026-08-03, same day)** — diagnosed item 98's specific failure first rather than just tuning parameters: its one failing walk-forward window (test period 2021-10 to 2022-11) had exactly **one trade** in 13 months, driving that window's whole Sharpe off a single -2.65% loss — thin evidence, not a real demonstration the mechanism breaks there. A natural "trade more often" fix (VIX9D/VIX instead of VIX/VIX3M, a faster-moving ratio) was tested honestly and made things clearly worse (Sharpe 0.71 → 0.21, max drawdown -30% → -46% despite 171 vs 63 trades) — reported and discarded, not kept in the codebase, rather than quietly moving on from a negative result.
    - **The angle that worked: stop trading the VIX ETP directly, use the term structure as a risk gate on strategies that already trade often enough for walk-forward to judge.** New `VixRegimeGatedStrategy` in `vix_term_structure.py` — same "wrap an inner strategy, mask entries only, exits always pass through" convention as `regime.py`/`macro_regime.py`'s existing gates, with `SmaCrossoverVixGatedStrategy` and `DonchianBreakoutVixGatedStrategy` gating the platform's two trend-following families to only trade while VIX/VIX3M is calm (contango). A structurally different bet from item 98 — not "harvest the vol premium," but "does sitting out stress regimes improve an already-proven trend strategy."
    - **A real negative sub-finding, reported honestly rather than glossed over**: the SMA-family gate turned out to be a complete no-op on both `SPY` and `QQQ` — byte-identical results to the ungated baseline, because SMA crossover's rare entries (12-15 over 10+ years) simply never happened to land during a backwardation period in this specific history. Confirmed directly by comparing gated vs. ungated stats line by line before trusting either.
    - **The breakout-family gate is where the hypothesis actually paid off.** `SPY/breakout_vixgated` **PROMOTE** — no blocking reasons, mean DSR 0.629, worst-OOS Sharpe +0.327 (positive across all 4 windows). Real before/after: return +60.9% → **+68.4%**, Sharpe 0.759 → **0.819**, drawdown unchanged at -8.6%, roughly the same 52-53 trades either side — a genuine, if modest, improvement from the gate, not noise. `QQQ/breakout_vixgated` clears every one of its own gates too (worst-OOS Sharpe +0.553, its own edge just as real) but lands on HOLD purely from the correlation gate (0.71 correlated with the higher-DSR `SPY` version, already selected) — the same "real edge, lost a tiebreak" situation as several other combos this session, not a failure of the underlying idea.
    - **Not yet paper-traded** — a fresh PROMOTE the same day it was found, same "propose, a human decides" principle as every other combo here; worth a look before adding it to `daily_paper_trade.sh`.

## How things actually work (conceptual notes, not just code)

*These are explanations worked through in conversation, not just build-log entries — captured here so the reasoning doesn't only live in chat history.*

**Paper trading mechanics.** Nothing runs unless invoked (manually, or by the launchd job). Each invocation: refresh the ticker's data → compute the strategy's signal across the full price history → look at only the *most recent bar's* signal value → compare to the currently open/flat paper position → open/close/hold accordingly → log it. Everything is fictional — no brokerage, no API call to any trading venue, a $10,000 fake starting balance tracked in a local, insert-only DuckDB table (`paper_trade_events`). Real limitation: since only the latest bar is checked, a **single-day pulse signal** (SMA crossover is true only on the exact crossover day, then false again) is silently missed forever if the check isn't run that specific day. Breakout-style signals are more persistent (can stay true several days in a row) so less fragile to this, but not immune.

**Why checking more often than daily wouldn't help, and what actually would need a rethink.** The strategies operate on daily bars — running the check every hour would just re-read the same unchanged number 23 times. A Mac mini fixes *reliability* (no more skipped days from a sleeping laptop) — it does not add accuracy or frequency benefit to what's built today. Genuine intraday trading is a separate, larger project: a different (usually paid, unlike free daily data) data source, strategies redesigned around a faster timeframe, and costs/slippage that matter far more at higher frequency. It's also not obviously *more profitable* — retail-scale high-frequency strategies typically fight thinner per-trade margins against costs and compete directly with professional HFT infrastructure, a fight the current daily-bar swing approach mostly avoids. Treat "go faster" as a deliberate, separate future initiative, not an upgrade that falls out of better hardware.

**News monitoring — why it's being deferred, not dismissed.** Public news is priced into liquid tickers (the ones in this universe) very quickly — a naive "watch the news, act on it" approach is not a free edge; if anything it's a crowded, professionally-contested one. Any news-derived signal must clear the *exact same bar* as the price-based strategies — backtested, walk-forward validated, DSR-corrected, risk-gated — not trusted just because it's "AI-powered" or real-time. It also needs a real, and likely paid, data-source decision before any building starts. Sequenced explicitly after the Mac mini (continuous polling shouldn't run on hardware that sleeps) and treated as its own project with its own validation, not an assumed extension of `research_cycle.py`.

**Governing principle: automation proposes, a human decides.** This has been the throughline of every automation feature built so far, worth stating once explicitly rather than leaving implicit: `daily_paper_trade.sh` hardcodes its combo list rather than deriving it from `promotion.py`; `research_cycle.py` reports newly-promoted/demoted combos but never adds or removes anything from paper trading itself; scheduling was only added after being explicitly asked for, and only for the one piece (paper trading) that was actually requested — `research_cycle.py`'s own scheduling was deliberately left undecided; `XLF/sma` sits as a flagged, pending decision rather than being auto-added the moment it cleared promotion. This is the practical, everyday version of the vision's "Governance" section (see [[Enhancement Ideas]]) applied at every layer of the system, not just the paper-to-live boundary it was originally written for.

## Known simplifications to revisit later (not bugs, just walking-skeleton scope cuts)

- Signals and execution both use the same bar's close — a common daily-bar backtest simplification, but not fully realistic (real fills would be next-bar). Position sizing's volatility estimate is shifted by 1 bar to avoid compounding this, but the underlying signal timing itself isn't next-bar yet.
- Param grids widened from 3 to 8 per strategy (item 18) — DSR's correction now has real material to work with, though still just the 3 existing strategy families' existing param shape, not new strategy invention.
- Only daily equity/ETF bars — 26 tickers pulled so far (original 14: SPY, QQQ, IWM, DIA, XLF, XLE, XLK, XLV, TLT, IEF, GLD, USO, EFA, EEM; added item 20: XLU, XLP, XLI, XLB, XLRE, XLC, MTUM, USMV, UUP; added item 27: XLY, HYG, LQD), spanning equities/sectors/factors/bonds/credit/commodities/international/currency, but no intraday and no futures/crypto yet.
- Risk gate is a single hard drawdown threshold (-25%) applied per strategy in isolation — no portfolio-level exposure/correlation limits yet, because there's no multi-strategy concurrent execution to limit.
- No live trading exists. Paper trading now runs daily via launchd (item 17), but only reliably while this Mac is awake at 17:00 — see the caveat there. "Continuous scanning" isn't meaningful anyway since the data layer is daily bars only — checking more than once/day would be a no-op.
- Still no *fully autonomous* agents (Research/Quant/Risk Manager/Portfolio Manager/Execution/Performance/Evolution/Supervisor from [[Vision & Mission]]) in the sense of acting on their own conclusions — `research_cycle.py` (item 18) is a manually-invoked, deterministic version of a slice of the Research/Evolution/Performance roles (widen search, re-validate, track verdict drift over time). The Research Agent (item 45) is the one piece that now genuinely runs on its own schedule and generates real new ideas unattended (LLM-driven strategy generation exists now, via free local Hermes3) — but it still only *proposes*: every candidate lands as `pending_human_review`, nothing it produces can reach `registry.py` or live trading without a person reading the code and deciding by hand.

Current real state: 50/503 tickers, 5,099 rows. Tomorrow's 06:30 run should resume on its own; worth a spot-check rather than assuming.
- **News-sentiment event-study scaffolding built 2026-08-13 — real infrastructure discovered along the way, not a from-scratch build.** User asked what other big things to work on; "news monitoring" was initially (and wrongly) proposed as a new project blocked on the Mac mini and a paid data source. Checking the actual codebase before scoping it found that's already half-built: `stock_agent.py` has been running hourly since 2026-07-25 (free Yahoo RSS headlines + local-Ollama sentiment scoring, see `data/stock_news.py`/`store/stock_agent.py`), accumulating real data at zero cost — 17,972 headlines across 85 liquid tickers, 6,073 scored `news_sentiment_shift` signals. It was deliberately built notification-only, though: the `stock_agent_signals` schema structurally has no `verdict`/`score`/`rank` column, by design, and nothing from it has ever gone through this platform's backtest/walk-forward/DSR pipeline.
  - **The real gap, and what was actually built**: `src/quant_platform/sentiment_hypotheses.py` + `scripts/sentiment_hypotheses_run.py`, structurally mirroring `congress_hypotheses.py` (per-event forward-excess-return panel, pre-registered hypotheses, Benjamini-Hochberg FDR correction) rather than the portfolio-simulation shape of `congress_copy.py` — sentiment shifts are sparse, cross-ticker, directional claims about individual events, the same shape of problem H1-H6 already solve for, not a portfolio-construction rule.
  - **Five hypotheses, fixed before running, not searched**: H1-H4 test whether positive-sentiment events outperform negative-sentiment events (excess over SPY) at each of 4 pre-registered short horizons (1/3/5/10 trading days — short on purpose, matching this project's own prior conclusion that liquid-ticker news prices in fast, not a PEAD-style multi-month drift question). H5 tests whether higher-magnitude shifts predict a bigger move in the predicted direction, at the middle (5-day) horizon.
  - **A real data-quality bug found and fixed before it could inflate results**: `stock_agent.py` runs hourly with a 3-hour news lookback and no cursor, so the same headline can be "the latest in the window" across 2-3 consecutive hourly runs, each one re-scoring it fresh (LLM scoring isn't guaranteed deterministic) and recording a new signal row ~1h apart. Confirmed this is real and large, not theoretical: deduplicating to the first occurrence per (ticker, headline_text) collapsed 6,073 raw signals to 2,736 real distinct events — 3,340 (55%) were duplicate re-scores of the same headline, not new information.
  - **A different lookahead-safety wrinkle from `congress_hypotheses.py`'s own convention, reasoned through rather than copy-pasted**: that module's `LAG_DAYS=1` exists specifically to push a bare `disclosure_date` (no time-of-day) forward to the next trading day. This module's events carry a real intraday `created_at` timestamp instead, which is almost never exactly midnight — so the shared `_exec_date()` helper's `searchsorted()` already lands on the next trading day with zero added lag. Reusing `LAG_DAYS=1` here would have silently doubled the delay to two trading days; set to `LAG_DAYS=0` instead, documented explicitly in the module docstring so this isn't rediscovered confused later.
  - **A `MIN_N_PER_GROUP=20` honesty gate**, since the feed is only ~3 weeks old: any hypothesis with fewer than 20 events on either side is reported as `UNDERPOWERED (not evaluated)` regardless of its p-value, rather than letting an early noisy split masquerade as a real verdict.
  - **Verified against real data, not just unit-tested**: ran the actual script end to end. Current honest result: H4 (10-day horizon) is underpowered (n=35/15); H1-H3 and H5 have adequate n (85-115) but all currently point the *wrong* direction (positive-sentiment events underperforming negative ones) with p-values nowhere near significant — unremarkable noise at this sample size, not a real finding, and the script correctly declines to call it one.
  - **Deliberately not run to a conclusion today** — same "patient" posture as the PEAD data pull. The feed keeps accumulating for free, unattended, via the existing hourly schedule; re-run `scripts/sentiment_hypotheses_run.py` periodically as real sample size builds rather than reading anything into today's numbers. `KNOWN_LIMITATIONS` in the module (no per-ticker clustered/dependence correction yet, LLM-scoring non-determinism, no transaction costs modeled) documented up front, same convention as `congress_hypotheses.py`'s own.

Research Agent had been crash-looping against the same corrupt file non-stop since then (confirmed via its own logs, identical error every cycle) -- stopped first via `launchctl bootout` before doing anything else, so nothing was actively hammering the bad file during diagnosis.

Preserved the corrupt live file by renaming it to `data/market.duckdb.corrupt_2026-08-09_2230` (not deleted, in case anything is ever worth forensically recovering from it), copied the verified Aug 9 06:00 backup into `data/market.duckdb`, then confirmed the restored file was genuinely healthy with a real read-write round trip (created a table, inserted a row, read it back, dropped it) -- not just that it could be opened read-only. Spot-checked that the specific work from the 2026-08-05 session survived intact (the 3 candidate rejections from that day still show `human_decision='rejected'`). Research Agent reloaded via `launchctl bootstrap` (it had been fully unloaded by the earlier `bootout`, not just paused) and confirmed running a clean cycle against the restored database with no further corruption errors.

Given this platform's own measured daily activity (most days: zero or one trade across every live combo), this is a small, acceptable loss, not a significant one.

- **New Research Agent gate: reject a strategy whose entry signal doesn't discriminate (2026-08-05) — real gap found and closed, not a hypothetical.** The "22,572-candidate backlog" flagged the day before turned out to be a misreading (`human_decision IS NULL` mostly means "auto-rejected before reaching a human," not "waiting" -- see `final_status`: only 115 of 24,306 candidates ever generated reached `pending_human_review`, 112 already decided, 3 genuinely pending). Reviewing those 3 pending `VolatilityRankStrategy` candidates (screened on `ASTS`, `RGC`, `GT-USD`) by hand found real code bugs in all three, none caught by lint/sandbox/backtest/generalization/stability: ASTS's high-or-low volatility check is a mathematical tautology (`rng > avg*0.5 OR rng < avg/0.5` is true for any positive value, since half the average is always less than double it) -- degenerates to a plain "price above its own 20-day average" filter; RGC compares a raw closing price directly against a raw dollar-range value (unit mismatch) -- one side permanently off, the other fires on 85% of days; GT-USD misuses pandas' `.rank()` against the *whole* multi-year history instead of a rolling window, so its 99.5th-percentile threshold only excludes the ~99 single calmest days ever, firing on 96% of days. All three are essentially "stay long almost permanently," not the selective volatility-timing idea they claim to test. **Added a new Stage 2a to `process_candidate()`** (`scripts/research_agent.py`, before the real backtest so a degenerate candidate never spends walk-forward's compute either): rejects if the entry signal fires on 0% or >30% of days. Threshold calibrated against real, correctly-working strategies in this codebase, not guessed -- measured directly: `sma` 0.4%, `rsi` 2.1%, `bollinger` 2.6%, `breakout` 14.4% (the highest of the four), all comfortably under 30%; the three broken candidates measured 49.8%/84.9%/96.1%. New `signal_sanity_passed`/`signal_sanity_detail` columns on `strategy_candidates` (JSON-serialized, same convention as `generalization_detail`). Verified end-to-end, not just unit-tested in isolation: ran the real `process_candidate()` against RGC's actual known-broken source (correctly rejected, `entry_rate=0.849`) and against a real working strategy (`DonchianBreakoutStrategy`, the closest legitimate case to the new threshold at 14.4%) confirming it passes through to the normal downstream gates unaffected (`entry_rate=0.145`, proceeded to and correctly failed on an unrelated benchmark check). Test rows cleaned up after. **Update, same day**: the 3 original pending candidates were left untouched while the gate was being built, then reviewed and rejected by the user once the gate work was done -- `human_decision='rejected'` recorded on all three (`cand_af4e6b9ef2`/ASTS, `cand_2a1ca5d9b0`/RGC, `cand_6feab45717`/GT-USD), each with a specific note citing its own bug, same convention as the dashboard's own reject endpoint (`agent_ecosystem.py`'s `update_candidate({"human_decision", "human_notes", "decided_at"})`). Zero candidates now sit in `pending_human_review` with a null decision -- the real, current backlog (as opposed to the misread 22,572 number) is fully caught up.
- **~~Dashboard's recurring "Unique file handle conflict" DB errors~~ FIXED 2026-08-04.** Noticed in passing while investigating the Ollama issue below (2,504 occurrences accumulated in `agent_ecosystem.py`'s stderr log, across two different endpoints). Root cause: the 4 `store/*.py` modules the dashboard actually uses (`candidates.py`, `graduation.py`, `paper.py`, `stock_agent.py`) each have their own `_connect()` helper (small, single-purpose, deliberately duplicated rather than shared -- established convention) that opens a connection, then runs schema-creation/migration DDL with **no exception safety** before returning it. Under `agent_ecosystem.py`'s `ThreadingHTTPServer`, two concurrent requests hitting the same table's `_connect()` can race on that DDL -- confirmed this is real and exercisable, not theoretical: a synthetic stress test (450 concurrent DDL calls across 30 rounds against a fresh table) produced 14 genuine `TransactionException: Catalog write-write conflict` failures, the same general class of concurrency exception as the original `BinderException: Unique file handle conflict`. Under the old code, whichever thread lost that race left its connection unclosed -- forever, for the rest of the process's life, since nothing ever called `.close()` on the error path. That's almost certainly why one transient race cascaded into thousands of repeat failures instead of clearing on its own (only a full process restart -- coincidentally what today's on-demand dashboard change also did -- would reset it). **Fixed** by wrapping each `_connect()`'s DDL/migration setup in try/except, closing the connection before re-raising on any failure -- verified three ways: (1) forcing a schema failure directly confirms the connection is actually closed, not just that a later call happens to succeed (a real leaked-and-referenced connection was tested separately and did *not* reproduce the collision either, confirming this is a narrow, real-concurrency-only race, not simple object lifetime); (2) the same 450-call/30-round concurrent stress test against the real, now-fixed schema DDL produced zero errors; (3) a clean batch immediately after the stress test also produced zero errors, confirming no cascading residual state. Honest limit: the exact original exception subtype (`BinderException`) wasn't forced to reproduce directly, only a same-class relative (`TransactionException`) -- the fix protects against both and any other similar one uniformly (catches `Exception` broadly, not one specific subtype), so this isn't treated as a gap.
- **Research Agent's Ollama connection failures — real root cause found, symptom fixed, underlying constraint not.** 15 crashes on 2026-08-04 alone, each silently discarding whatever candidate was mid-generation (`research_agent_loop.sh`'s outer `while true` just moved on 5s later, so the pipeline never stalled, but real generation throughput was lost). Root cause confirmed, not guessed: this Mac mini runs 16GB RAM total, and at the time of investigation was sitting at ~8.9GB of 9.2GB swap in use, ~59MB free physical RAM -- Ollama's `llama-server` alone holds ~5.5GB resident, `agent_ecosystem.py` (the persistent dashboard) another ~895MB, on top of whatever else is running (browser, this Claude Code session, Obsidian, etc.). Under that pressure a local loopback connection to Ollama can get reset mid-request when either side gets paged out -- a transient infra hiccup, not a bad LLM response (`llm_client.py`'s own docstring is explicit that a malformed *response* is never retried, on purpose; this is a different failure class, before any response exists at all). **Fixed**: `_ollama_chat()` now retries a connection failure with exponential backoff + jitter (4 attempts, 1s->2s->4s, capped at 8s) -- the exact same shape as `db.py`'s own lock-retry convention, reused rather than reinvented. Verified both paths directly: a normal call still works unchanged, and a forced failure (pointed at a dead port) retries 3 times with visibly growing delays before raising a clear final error. **Honest limit of this fix**: it makes the *symptom* (a crashed, lost candidate) self-heal for ordinary transient blips -- it does not fix the *cause* (16GB RAM genuinely being tight for what's running on this machine). If failures keep recurring after this, the real fix is fewer concurrent heavy processes or more RAM, not more retries.
- **Meta-labeling Phase 0 — real, mixed result; not yet a clean go for Phase 1.** Scoped and built 2026-08-04 (`scripts/meta_labeling_phase0.py`), following straight from the portfolio-allocation scoping entry below finding live combos too thin to allocate across yet. Checked trade counts directly before building anything: individual live combos have far too few trades to fit or validate a classifier (`QQQ/sma`'s entire backtest history is 12 trades, `QQQ/breakout` is 53) — pooled DonchianBreakoutStrategy across the full 32-ticker `research_cycle.py` universe gets to 1,640 trades, SmaCrossoverStrategy to 572, which is the only workable scope. Features reuse existing gate logic as continuous inputs instead of hard thresholds (Kaufman's Efficiency Ratio from `regime.py`, realized vol, volume ratio, VIX term-structure ratio from `vix_term_structure.py`), all computed strictly as of each trade's own entry date. Validation: a strict date-based walk-forward split (train only before a cutoff, test only after) plus a placebo baseline (does the filter beat randomly keeping the same *number* of trades, 2,000 draws) — beating "take every trade" was deliberately not the bar, since almost any filter clears that by chance; beating the placebo is the real question.
  - **Breakout (first, pre-committed run, the bigger pool)**: filtered result beat the placebo on win_rate/mean_return/sharpe_like at the 80.5/80.8/82.8th percentile — real but short of the ~95% conventional-significance bar. Read as "not enough," consistent with how this project has closed out other marginal results (short-interest, insider-copying).
  - **SMA (second, disclosed check — one pre-committed re-run, not open-ended fishing, same discipline as item 92's FDR correction)**: a materially stronger result — 98.0/99.7/96.2nd percentile. But two real caveats stated plainly rather than just reporting the good number: (1) this is the second of exactly two tests, and a Bonferroni-style correction (matching how this project already treats multiple-testing everywhere else) would raise the real bar to ~97.5% for family-wise 5% significance — win_rate and mean_return still clear that raised bar, sharpe_like (96.2%) doesn't quite; (2) the SMA test set is smaller (172 trades, 89 kept) than breakout's (491/272), so more per-trade sampling noise sits under this number.
  - **The two families disagree with each other, which is itself informative**: if the underlying features (trend strength, vol, VIX regime) genuinely predicted signal quality as a general market phenomenon, both strategy families would likely show at least directionally similar strength, not one marginal and one strong. Doesn't prove SMA's result is spurious, but it's a real reason for caution rather than declaring victory on the stronger number alone.
  - **Widened SMA to the real, decisive test — added `--universe wide` (queries already-stored data for every non-crypto/futures ticker clearing the platform's standard $5M/day liquidity bar + 2016+ history, same convention as `universe_promote_triage.py`; no new fetches needed) — 2,057 tickers, 45,074 pooled trades, 13,521 in the out-of-sample test set. This resolved the small-sample ambiguity, but not the way a clean "yes" would have: win_rate and mean_return both clear the placebo decisively (100.0% and 99.2nd percentile), but the risk-adjusted (Sharpe-like) measure does not — it lands at only the 33.6th percentile, *worse* than a random draw more often than not. Read plainly: the filter finds trades that pay more on average, not trades that are actually higher-quality — it leans toward keeping signals during stronger-trend/higher-VIX conditions (the two features with the largest model weights), which produces bigger wins *and* bigger losses, not a more reliable edge. **Final word for now, user's explicit call**: sitting with this result as-is rather than iterating toward a version that also fixes the risk-adjusted number. Not built into Phase 1, not wired into live paper trading or the promotion pipeline — `scripts/meta_labeling_phase0.py` stays a prints-only research script, uncommitted. Worth revisiting only if someone wants to explicitly target risk-adjusted quality (e.g. a variance-aware feature or label) rather than raw return — not pursued here.

Phase 1 (done): fixed `blend_paper_trading.py`'s `DEFAULT_COMBOS` to the actual live 4-combo roster (was still the original 3, including the long-pulled `XLP/bollinger`), and fixed a real crash in `inverse_vol_weights()` — a combo that's never traded has a perfectly flat curve (zero realized volatility, not genuinely riskless, just no data yet), which divided by zero into `nan` weights. Now skips inverse-vol with a clear explanation when any leg is still flat, rather than crashing or printing a misleading number. **Honest result running it for real**: only `QQQ/breakout_ls` has ever entered a position (since 2026-07-24, still open) — `QQQ/sma`, `QQQ/breakout`, and `FXI/bollinger_regime` are all still flat/HOLD since inception. The "blended account" today is really just that one position diluted 4x by uninvested cash in the other three legs (-0.37% over 7 trading days) — there isn't yet enough real live activity to say whether the backtest-level diversification benefit (item 26: Sharpe 1.01 blended vs 0.67/0.73/0.95 individually) is showing up for real; re-run once more combos have actually fired. **Two bigger, undecided phases flagged, not started**: phase 2 is whether/how to fold BTC/ETH funding-arb into the same view (a leveraged margin/notional carry trade, not a simple $10k spot curve — blending naively risks comparing apples to oranges; leaning toward reporting it as a separate sleeve rather than unifying without a principled risk-normalization). - **~~Separately, from item 94: `FFAI`'s data has a real yfinance split-adjustment gap~~ FIXED 2026-08-04.** `data/fetch.py` now requests `actions=True` on its existing batched `yf.download()` calls (same request, no extra API cost) and runs a new `_backfill_unadjusted_splits()` pass: for each `Stock Splits` event in the fetched window, compares a 20-trading-day median close before vs. after the split, on a log scale, against which hypothesis it's closer to — "Yahoo already backfilled this" (ratio ~1) or "still needs correcting" (ratio ~ the reported split ratio itself). A single adjacent-day comparison was tried first and rejected: FFAI had a genuine ~96x speculative spike two trading days *before* its own 2026-07-24 split, which fooled a naive day-pair check into reading real volatility as "already adjusted" — the 20-day median is robust to that. Verified against FFAI's full real split history (2023-08-28, 2024-03-01, 2024-08-19 all correctly read as already-backfilled and left untouched; only the 11-day-old split got corrected) and against NVDA's clean, unambiguous 10-for-1 2024 split to confirm the correction's direction. Re-ran `fetch_and_store(['FFAI'])` to repair the 1,485 already-stored rows in place. **The corrected numbers are extreme but real, not a fix artifact**: pre-split closes now carry a ×150 factor consistent with the reverse split, and the day-over-day return right after the split is a genuine ~-99.6% (the stock's post-split price didn't hold anywhere near the pump-implied value) — both figures reflect real compounding price action (a speculative pump into the split, a crash right after), confirmed by checking that relative returns within each unadjusted segment are unchanged by the correction, only the absolute scale shifted. Not universe-wide re-validated (FFAI itself isn't a live PROMOTE, so no paper-traded combo was affected) — the fix is now permanent in `fetch.py` and will self-apply to any other ticker with an unbackfilled recent split on its next fetch, not just FFAI.

Re-verified directly 2026-08-04 (hostname `Mac-mini.local`, all jobs `launchctl list`-visible, code/DB paths local). **One real reliability gap found and fixed in the process**: the 06:00 `dbbackup` job's final `mv` (renaming the verified `.tmp` backup into its trusted dated filename) hit the same transient iCloud-sync-daemon folder lock already documented for the log-append step, silently leaving a stranded `.tmp` and no dated backup for 2026-08-04 until manually caught and completed — `scripts/backup_database.sh` now retries that rename (5 attempts, 2s apart) instead of failing the whole run on one transient lock. **One gap still open, needs the user directly**: auto-login isn't enabled, so an unattended reboot leaves every scheduled job dead until someone logs in at the physical console — fix is in System Settings and needs the account password, which isn't something to hand over or do on the user's behalf.

**Update 2026-08-05: the 5-attempt/2s retry above wasn't actually enough — real root cause found, fix widened.** The self-cleaning `backupcheck` diagnostic (see the launchd-migration entry a few lines below) caught the exact same `mv` failure again the very next morning. This time confirmed the real cause directly via `brctl status` during a live failure, not just suspected: the *previous* day's ~3.86GB backup still had an active iCloud upload in progress at the exact moment the new rename ran — a genuinely long-running background task (brctl showed a "cleanup" window up to ~an hour), not a brief blip. An 8-second retry budget was never going to be enough for that. Widened to 16 attempts at 30s apart (~8 minutes total), comfortably inside the 10-minute gap before `backupcheck`'s own 06:10 verification run. Verified the retry loop's logic in isolation (succeeds once the underlying condition clears; correctly fails loudly with a non-zero exit if it never does within budget, rather than hanging or silently succeeding) rather than waiting a real 8 minutes end-to-end. Manually rescued both 2026-08-04's and 2026-08-05's stranded `.tmp` files the same way as before while this was being fixed.

**Same day, checking whether the widened retry actually held up caught a second, unrelated real bug**: the self-cleaning `backupcheck` diagnostic's *own* cleanup never ran on 2026-08-05 -- both its script and its LaunchAgent plist were still sitting on disk hours later, even though `launchctl list` showed the job already gone from launchd's registry. Root cause: the script called `launchctl bootout` on its *own* label while it was still that label's running process -- launchd can (and apparently did) kill the process immediately as a side effect of successfully unloading it, before the two `rm -f` lines that came after it in the script ever ran. Fixed by reordering: both file deletions now happen *before* `bootout`, including `rm -f "$0"` on the still-executing script itself (verified directly this is safe on macOS -- an already-open/running process keeps executing fine after its own file is unlinked). A fresh one-shot check for 2026-08-06 06:10 is loaded with the corrected ordering, to verify both the widened retry itself and that this check's own cleanup now works.

**2026-08-13 — full post-recovery take-stock audit, everything checked, nothing assumed.** Session resumed after the corruption/restore above; user asked for a thorough health check before trusting the platform again, with no time pressure. Findings:
- **Database**: every one of the 30 tables read cleanly (no further corruption anywhere else in the file), a forced `CHECKPOINT` completed without error, and spot-checked duplicate-key queries on `ohlcv_daily`/`strategy_candidates` came back clean. As thorough a health verification as reasonably possible without specialized DuckDB internals tooling.
- **Real gap found and fixed**: `daily_paper_trade.sh`'s `RunAtLoad=true` fired automatically the moment the Mac logged back in, *before* the DB restore -- every step failed against the still-corrupt file, but the wrapper script doesn't propagate individual step failures to its own exit code, so launchd recorded a clean "last exit code = 0" despite nothing real happening. Manually re-ran the whole thing after the restore: SPY refresh succeeded (2,920 rows), all 4 core combos checked cleanly (`QQQ/breakout` opened a new long, `QQQ/breakout_ls` closed one leg at -3.79% and opened another), and the full ~50-candidate approved-paper-trading loop ran end to end with a healthy mix of real wins and losses (VRTX +6.99%, ASTS +1.50%, STFS +1.82% against LTC-USD -3.91%, LCID -1.64%, SUNC -1.97%, MANE -1.40%) -- exactly what a working, unbiased system should look like, not a red flag either direction. Portfolio drawdown check at the end: -1.48% on the blended book, 0% on both funding-arb legs, no breaches.
- **Funding-arb specifically checked, not just assumed fine**: both BTC and ETH re-synced cleanly (a real DNS resolution failure in the Aug 9 log turned out to be a transient network outage during the move, not a script bug) -- both showing healthy positive cumulative funding P&L (+$28 BTC, +$21 ETH), `ACCRUED` action, no risk events.
- **Three tickers show `STALE_DATA`** in the approved-candidate loop (SHPG, AFSI, DVMT) -- checked and explained, not a new problem: all three trace to real, years-old corporate actions (Shire's 2019 Takeda acquisition, AmTrust's 2018 go-private, Dell's 2018 tracking-stock conversion), so their stored price history correctly stops at that point. The script's own `STALE_DATA` label is working as intended, catching exactly this case rather than silently trading on ancient data.
- **Backup housekeeping closed out**: renamed the Aug 9 06:00 `.tmp` (the file the restore was built from) to its proper dated name now that it's confirmed good, and ran a fresh manual backup of today's restored state rather than waiting for tomorrow's 06:00 slot -- both verified. Current retained set: Aug 3/4/5/9/13, well inside the 7-day KEEP limit.
- **Resources**: 0MB swap in use (fresh boot from the move), 97GB free disk, no memory pressure -- much healthier than the swap-thrashing state found earlier in the project's history.
- **Housekeeping**: moved the preserved corrupt file (`market.duckdb.corrupt_2026-08-09_2230`) out of the repo entirely, to `Trading/quarantine/`, after noticing it didn't match `.gitignore`'s `data/*.duckdb` pattern (git showed it as untracked) -- avoids ever risking it getting staged, without deleting it.
- **Code**: confirmed clean and fully pushed, no drift.
- **~~The iCloud backup-lock issue~~ retry window widened a third time, 2026-08-13 -- this time sized from evidence, not another guess.** Two prior widenings (Aug 4: ~8s; Aug 5: ~8min) both still failed on their very next real test (Aug 5 and Aug 9 respectively). Rather than pick a third arbitrary number, reused the hard evidence already gathered: `brctl status` during the Aug 5 failure showed the stuck upload's own "cleanup" window running up to ~59 minutes. Set the budget to 70 attempts at 60s apart (~70 minutes total) -- real margin above the observed worst case, not another partial fix. Cost is low: by the time this loop runs, the DB copy/verify is already done and the source file released, so a long wait just means the shell script sits idle in sleep, nothing else is blocked. Verified the loop logic in isolation again (same pattern as both prior fixes, still holds with the new numbers). Noted directly in the script: if a one-shot verification check like `backupcheck` is ever set up again, it needs to check meaningfully later than 07:10, not 06:10 -- that gap was sized for the old 8-minute budget. Whether this actually holds up won't be known until it's tested against a real slow morning -- not verified end-to-end, just the most evidence-based version so far.
- **Update 2026-08-13, later same day: checked whether the missing 08-06/07/08/10-12 snapshots are actually recoverable — they're not, and that's now confirmed rather than assumed.** User asked to backfill the gap in the retained backup set (only 08-03/04/05/09/13 exist against a 7-day `KEEP` window). Declined to just copy today's verified DB under those older dated filenames without asking first — a snapshot named `market_2026-08-06.duckdb` that actually contains 08-13's data would look exactly as trustworthy as a real one while being silently wrong, the same failure class the `COPY FROM DATABASE`/verify-before-rename rework was built to eliminate. Checked both real recovery paths before concluding there's nothing to backfill from: `tmutil destinationinfo` confirms Time Machine has never been configured on this machine, and there's no iCloud file-version history to fall back on either, because the files never existed under those names in the first place — `logs/backup_verification_2026-08-05.log` and `..._2026-08-06.log` both show the same `mv: ... Operation not permitted` failure recurring on 08-06/07/08/10/11/12, from *before* today's 70-attempt retry (7b7e687) existed, when the script had no rename retry at all and just died leaving an orphaned `.tmp`. Nothing was ever captured for those six days to later recover — this is a real, permanent, already-bounded gap (consistent with the smaller 08-09 gap already written up above), not a new loss. Leaving the retained set to refill naturally over the next week under `KEEP=7`; no action taken on the files themselves.
